# Ledger: EFX₀ with at most three relevant goods per agent

The single source of truth. A status changes only in a pull request that adds the artifact in the Artifact column; CI checks that every PROVED, CERTIFIED or REFUTED row points to files that exist. The Lean column names machine-checked statements in `lean/` (core Lean, no `sorry`, standard axioms only; see `lean/README.md`); CI checks that each has a `#print axioms` certificate and that `lean/check.sh` passes.

| ID | Claim | Status | Artifact | Lean | Notes |

| L12 | Real values reduce to natural numbers: for nonnegative real additive valuations v in which every agent has ≤ 3 relevant goods, there are natural-number additive valuations w with the same relevant goods such that v_i(S) ≤ v_i(T) ⟺ w_i(S) ≤ w_i(T) for every agent i and all sets S, T; so an allocation is EFX₀ for w iff it is for v (same bundles, so the D shape carries over), and balance and cores are preserved | PROVED | `proofs/real_values.md`, `lean/EFX/RealValues.lean` | `EFX.l12`, `EFX.OrderedValue.exists_agree`, `EFX.OrderedValue.tri_rep`, `EFX.numRelevant_eq_of_agree`, `EFX.OrderedValue.balanced_iff_of_agree`, `EFX.efx0_iff_of_agree` | per agent: after cancelling the common goods, every comparison is between disjoint subsets of the ≤ 3 relevant goods a ≥ b ≥ c > 0; since b < a + c and c < a + b always, the order is fixed by the ties among a, b, c and the sign of a − (b + c); one natural-number representative per pattern (e.g. (4, 3, 2), (3, 2, 1), (5, 2, 1), (2, 2, 1), (3, 2, 2), (2, 1, 1), (3, 1, 1), (1, 1, 1), (2, 1), (1, 1), (1)). Extends `EFX.target` and `EFX.LB.corollaryD` (natural-number values) to real values. Machine-checked in Lean (`formal/real-values`, `lean/EFX/RealValues.lean`) for values in any type satisfying `EFX.OrderedValue` (0, +, ≤: + associative and commutative with identity 0, ≤ a total order, a ≤ b ⟺ a + c ≤ b + c; ℝ≥0, ℚ≥0, ℕ satisfy these), nonnegative values as a hypothesis: `EFX.l12` gives w with the same relevant goods and the same answer to every comparison between two subset sums (`EFX.Agree`); the per-agent pattern argument is `EFX.OrderedValue.exists_agree`, with the eight representatives (permuted, 31 triples) checked by `decide +kernel` (`EFX.Pat.table`) and the phantom-slot trick for agents with one or two relevant goods; EFX₀, the relevant-goods count and balance transfer (`EFX.efx0_iff_of_agree`, `EFX.numRelevant_eq_of_agree`, `EFX.OrderedValue.balanced_iff_of_agree`). The remark that cores are preserved (K1–K4) is written only (`proofs/real_values.md`), not formalized |
| S2.A | Collisions vs. P-capacity (plan Step 2): an EFX₀ allocation with all bundles ≤ 2 and e empty bundles leaves ≥ δ contested tops alone, so δ ≤ σ − 2e (δ = #contested tops − P-capacity, σ = 2n − m) | PROVED | `proofs/construction.md` | | §2. Necessary only, and never the binding reason for n ≤ 5: every C2-failing profile there has δ ≤ σ; what forces the large bundle is second-order (losers that need their b alone too) |
| S2.S | Construction LB is sound: any allocation it returns is EFX₀ with ≤ 1 bundle of > 2 goods; that bundle has #NA − σ + 2 goods (NA = the goods some agent needs alone) | PROVED | `proofs/construction.md`, `src/construct.py` | `EFX.LB.lb_sound_model`, `EFX.LB.lb_sound`, `EFX.LB.sound` | §3, Theorem 1 (soundness) and Lemma 2 (size of the large bundle), directly from the EFX₀ definition (no L5). LB can fail only in its last step (no owner for the overflow bundle). Lean: Theorem 1 (not Lemma 2), both in the abstract form (`EFX.LB.sound`: picks with (I1), upgraded/frozen/slot-filled bundles and the owner constraint ⟹ EFX₀ for every consistent valuation, ties allowed, ≤ 1 large bundle) and for LB itself (`EFX.LB.lb_sound_model`), which is defined in Lean as in `construct.py` except that Phase 1's processing order is an argument: the theorem holds for every order, so LB's order heuristic is not formalized. That the Lean `lb` computes what `construct.py` computes is evidence: same allocation on 1,809,209 core–profile pairs (all with n ≤ 4, samples with n = 5, 6; `lean/scripts/lb_crosscheck.py`, `lean/scripts/lb_crosscheck.log`) |
| S2.R | Owner r (Theorem A): after any run of LB's Phase 1 (any choices at R1 and insertion steps) and LB's upgrades (in any order), if the last-processed agent r that is not upgraded is not a valid owner of the overflow bundle (no set H satisfies Lemma 1's condition), then the "bad case" holds: an agent k of r's block (a block leader) picked its top, {b_k, c_k} ⊆ J ∪ {Y_r}, k is frozen, the junk parts of the pairs exposed for r are pairwise disjoint, and every need chain from k ends at r | PROVED | `proofs/lb_last_step.md`, `lean/EFX/OwnerR.lean`, `lean/EFX/Blocks.lean`, `lean/EFX/LBPlus.lean`, `src/lbplus.c`, `src/lbplus.py`, `results/lbplus.log` | `EFX.LB.theoremA_invalid`, `EFX.LB.validOwner_iff`, `EFX.LB.theoremA`, `EFX.LB.kstar_spec`, `EFX.LB.exposed_lead`, `EFX.LB.phase1_run`, `EFX.LB.upFinal_valid`, `EFX.LB.state_of_final` | written proof: `proofs/lb_last_step.md` (Theorem A, §4); two independent reviews found no error. Machine-checked in Lean (`formal/lbplus`, PR #18), stated over the Lean definitions of LB's state (Blocks/OwnerR/PreAlloc/LBPlus), which a reader must check against the text; any run of Phase 1 and the upgrades gives the state Theorem A assumes (`EFX.LB.phase1_run`, `EFX.LB.upFinal_valid`, `EFX.LB.state_of_final`). "Valid owner" is Lemma 1's condition for some H (`EFX.LB.ValidOwner`); it holds exactly when an explicit hitting set (one good per exposed pair, a shared good for one meeting pair) fits (`EFX.LB.validOwner_iff`), so the test is computable. k is the agent exposed for r in r's block; that this is the last block ((A2)) is not needed and not formalized. Exposed agents are block leaders, one per block, and need chains inject them into slot-holding agents. Cross-check (evidence): `src/lbplus.c` asserts every step on all runs of S2.LB+, 0 failures |
| S2.LB+ | Construction LB⁺ (LB's Phase 1 with any choices at R1 and insertion steps, LB's upgrades in any order, owner r if r is a valid owner, or else one rotation along any need chain from k to r and owner k; any completions satisfying (OC)) never meets an undefined case (an output always exists, and in the rotation branch every need chain from k to r gives one), and every allocation it returns is EFX₀ with at most one bundle of > 2 goods, for every instance in which every agent values exactly three goods and is balanced | PROVED | `proofs/lb_last_step.md`, `lean/EFX/LBPlus.lean`, `lean/EFX/Rotation.lean`, `lean/EFX/PreAlloc.lean`, `lean/EFX/Blocks.lean`, `src/lbplus.c`, `src/lbplus.py`, `results/lbplus.log` | `EFX.LB.lbPlusRun_sound`, `EFX.LB.lbPlusOut_exists`, `EFX.LB.lbPlusOut_exists_chain`, `EFX.LB.lbPlus_sound_model`, `EFX.LB.lbPlus_sound`, `EFX.LB.theoremB`, `EFX.LB.Valid.sound` | written proof: `proofs/lb_last_step.md` (Theorems 1′, B, C); two independent reviews found no error. Machine-checked in Lean (`formal/lbplus`, PR #18), stated over the Lean definitions of LB's state (Blocks/OwnerR/PreAlloc/LBPlus), which a reader must check against the text: Theorem 1′ for every completion of a valid pre-allocation that satisfies (OC) (`EFX.LB.Valid.sound`), Theorem B for any need chain (`EFX.LB.theoremB`), and Theorem C for LB⁺ over all its choices (`EFX.LB.LBPlusRun`: every output is EFX₀ for every valuation consistent with the rankings, a ≥ b ≥ c > 0 and a ≤ b + c, ties allowed, with ≤ 1 large bundle, `EFX.LB.lbPlusRun_sound`; an output always exists, `EFX.LB.lbPlusOut_exists`; in the rotation branch every need chain from k* to r gives an output, `EFX.LB.lbPlusOut_exists_chain`) and for the computable `EFX.LB.lbPlus` (one of the outputs; `decide` examples in `lean/EFX/LBPlus.lean`). Does not use connectivity, the private-good condition, L5 or any computation. Cross-check (evidence, `results/lbplus.log`): LB⁺ run literally by `src/lbplus.c`, every lemma asserted, every output checked against the raw EFX₀ definition. Covers every core with n ≤ 6 under every ranking profile and every sequence of insertion choices (2.47 × 10⁹ runs, 370,342 rotations). It also covers LB's own runs and random insertion/R1 choices on every connected core with n = 7, 11 ≤ m ≤ 14 and n = 8, m ∈ {15, 16}, plus random non-core instances with n = 5–7: 5.84 × 10⁹ runs in all, 1,327,101 rotations, 0 failures. The independent Python implementation `src/lbplus.py` gives identical tallies on all 40 levels with n ≤ 5 |

| K3.ALG.TIME | K3ALG performs at most 400·(n + m + 1)⁴ counted operations on every instance with n ≥ 1 agents and m goods (any number of relevant goods), in the cost model of `proofs/k3_algorithm.md` §6 | PROVED | `proofs/k3_algorithm.md`, `lean/EFX/K3CostBound.lean`, `lean/EFX/Timed.lean`, `lean/EFX/K3CostLB.lean` | `EFX.K3.algoC_cost`, `EFX.K3.algoC_cost'`, `EFX.K3.lbPlusC_cost`, `EFX.K3.reduceC_cost`, `EFX.Timed.mkTable_cost` | Also ≤ 6400·(n + m)⁴ (`EFX.K3.algoC_cost'`). Cost model: the algorithm is written in a cost monad (`EFX.Timed`), and its value is `algo`, so the bound is for the program that computes the allocation. One unit is one of: a list step (with its comparison), a comparison of agents or goods, a table read, an input read, a comparison or addition of naturals (arbitrary size counts as one unit). Tables are arrays filled once; straight-line code is charged by annotated `tick`s. The dominant term is LB's upgrade loop. The finer O(n⁴ + n²m) is machine-checked (row K3.ALG.FINE). Written, not machine-checked: the O((n + m) log n) implementation `fast` of `k3/k3algo.py` (§5) |

Dominant: LB's upgrade loop, a⁴ + 17a³ + a²b + … (`EFX.K3.lbUpC_cost_fine`: ≤ a rounds × a candidates × (NA of one good by scanning the agents with a membership test in U, plus c_k ∈ J)). The surrogate is L12 made constructive (`EFX.K3.agree_surrogate`; `EFX.l12` itself is classical): per agent, one call v_i(g) ≤ 0 per good, then the twelve basic comparisons of its first three relevant goods (phantom slots for one or two), looked up in the 31-row table `EFX.Pat.reps` (the paper's proof sorts and uses at most six comparisons; same O(nm) calls). It is stored as an n × m table and read at unit cost, as `algoC` reads its input; `EFX.K3.algoOrd_eq`: the run is `EFX.K3.algo` on it. Cost: with c units per oracle call the surrogate costs ≤ c·n(m + 12) + 10nm + 971n (`EFX.K3.surrogateC_cost`, so ≤ n(m + 12) calls); with c = 1 the whole run costs ≤ n(m + 12) + 10nm + 971n + 400(n + m + 1)⁴ counted operations on every instance with n ≥ 1 (`EFX.K3.algoOrdC_cost`). The scaling (agent i's values times the product D_i > 0 of their denominators) preserves every comparison between two subset sums of one agent's values, hence the relevant goods and EFX₀, and K3ALG on the scaled values returns an allocation that is EFX₀ for the rational values whenever every agent positively values at most three goods | PROVED | `lean/EFX/K3Extras.lean` | `EFX.K3.algoRat_efx0`, `EFX.ratScale_agree`, `EFX.agree_scaleNat`, `EFX.scaleNat_cast`, `EFX.ratScale_val`, `EFX.ratScale_relevant`, `EFX.ratScale_numRelevant`, `EFX.ratScale_efx0_iff` | Rationals are core Lean's `Rat`, with an `EFX.OrderedValue Rat` instance, so the instance is an `EFX.OInst Rat` (the model mirrored in `lean/EFX/RealValues.lean`); comparisons of subset sums are `EFX.Agree`. `EFX.K3.algoRat I hn := EFX.K3.algo (EFX.ratScale I) hn`. The scaled value is `num(v) · (D_i / den(v))`, which equals v · D_i (`scaleNat_cast`); the product of the denominators is used, not their lcm (any common positive multiple works). Not formalized: the paper's remark that every decision of K3ALG is the same before and after scaling (K3ALG is defined on ℕ only), and the cost of computing the scaling |

| K4.TIE | Ties reduce to strict types: if every strict core profile of a k = 4 core has an EFX₀ allocation, every tied one does | PROVED | `k4/SCOUT.md` | `EFX.tie_reduction`, `EFX.core_reduction4_strict`, `EFX.target4_of_strict_cores` | closure argument with a perturbation supported on R_i, `k4/SCOUT.md` §2. Lean (`formal/k4-reduction`, `lean/EFX/K4Ties.lean`, over ℕ): `EFX.tie_reduction`: if every profile w with the same relevant goods as a connected k = 4 core v that is itself a connected k = 4 core and strict has an EFX₀ allocation, so does v; `EFX.Strict`: two disjoint sets of goods an agent values equally are both worthless to it (⟺ disjoint nonempty S, T ⊆ R_i have v_i(S) ≠ v_i(T)). The ε-perturbation and closedness become the integer perturbation M·v + w, M = 2^|M|, w_i(g) = 2^(goods after g) on R_i (`EFX.tieBreak`); an EFX₀ allocation for it is EFX₀ for v (`EFX.efx0_of_tieBreak`). `EFX.core_reduction4_strict` / `EFX.target4_of_strict_cores` (model): with K4.CORE, TARGET₄ for ≤ N agents follows from EFX₀ for connected strict k = 4 cores with ≤ N agents and a 4-good agent. Scope: the existing certificates discharge this hypothesis for N ≤ 4 (K4.R3 with K4.R4); K4.R5 covers n = 5 only with at most two 4-good agents, which the "every core with ≤ N agents" form cannot use. The discharge also relies on two steps outside Lean: relabeling list cores to the certificates' indices, and K4.OT's grid completeness (every strict type has a representative in the searched grid). Lemma 2₄ is stated with the hypothesis that the agent has at most 4 relevant goods, and shows more: the agent does not envy the owner's bundle; `EFX.LB4.selfProtect_seq` covers slots filled one agent at a time, whatever else is placed; with 5 relevant goods the value argument fails (`EFX.LB4.selfProtect_five`), and `EFX.LB4.Ex5.counterexample` (from the independent audit of PR #31) is an instance of every other hypothesis of `EFX.LB4.selfProtect` in which the agent is threatened, both by `decide`. Non-vacuity, by `decide`: `EFX.LB4.Ex.sound` (an owner's bundle of 3 goods; valid only with the owner's needs from its bundle) and `EFX.LB4.ExB.sound` (`EFX.LB4.Valid.sound`'s hypotheses with the owner's needs from its base, a frozen agent, a two-good base, a filled slot and a 3-good owner's bundle). What remains: C₄∃ for one-4-good-agent cores (K4.C4.1), and for K4.LB4 a rule for the insertion sequence or unboundedly many rotations with a potential |

| K4.IND.STEP | Conditional induction step (`k4/induct.md` §3, Theorem 4). 𝒟_j denotes the instances with ≤ 4 relevant goods per agent and ≤ j four-good agents. (b) Suppose PS(I − p, w) holds for every connected strict k = 4 core I ∈ 𝒟_{j+1}, every 4-good agent w of I and every good p private to w. Then a counterexample to TARGET₄ among all instances with ≤ j + 1 four-good agents, with the fewest agents and then the fewest goods, is a connected k = 4 core, may be taken with strict types, and has no 4-good agent with a private good (all its 4-good agents are Q4) | PROVED | `k4/induct.md` | | proof/k4-induct. | K4.STRAT.DL2.LEAN | Step 4 of `k4/strategy.md` §3 in Lean: DL_R ⟹ TARGET₄ for every neighbourhood relation R on pre-allocations; in particular DL₂ ⟹ TARGET₄. DL_R (`EFX.C4min.DefLocal R`): on every strict profile of every connected k = 4 core with ω = f − (2n − m) ≥ 1, every P ∈ 𝒫 with the fewest frozen agents and def(P) > 0 (+∞ included) has a P′ ∈ 𝒫 with the fewest frozen agents, R(P, P′) and def(P′) < def(P) (in ℤ ∪ {+∞}, read through `EFX.C4min.DeficitLE`: some integer d has def(P′) ≤ d and not def(P) ≤ d); DL₂ (`EFX.C4min.DefLocal2`) is R = "the bases of at most two agents differ" (`EFX.C4min.BasesDiffer` 2). By finite descent on the deficit, DL_R on an instance gives a pre-allocation of 𝒫 with the fewest frozen agents and def(P) ≤ 0; so DL_R ⟹ `C4minROConn` ⟹ TARGET₄ (`EFX.C4min.target4_of_C4minROConn`), and DL_R on every core, connected or not, ⟹ `TheoremC4minRO`. On the way: the deficit is an element of ℤ ∪ {+∞} (def(P) ≥ −2n, a finite deficit is attained); ω(P) = f(P) − (2n − m) on 𝒫, the same for every min-frozen P, and def(P) > 0 forces ω(P) ≥ 1; for the full relation, DL_R on an instance is equivalent to C₄ᵐⁱⁿ's removal-only conclusion there; widening R weakens DL_R | PROVED | `lean/EFX/C4minDescent.lean` | `EFX.C4min.exists_removalOnly_of_defLocalAt`, `EFX.C4min.C4minROConn_of_defLocal`, `EFX.C4min.target4_of_defLocal`, `EFX.C4min.C4minROConn_of_defLocal2`, `EFX.C4min.target4_of_defLocal2`, `EFX.C4min.C4minRO_of_defLocalAll`, `EFX.C4min.defLocalAt_top_iff`, `EFX.C4min.DefLocal.mono`, `EFX.C4min.basesDiffer_mono`, `EFX.C4min.deficitLT_iff`, `EFX.C4min.exists_least_deficit`, `EFX.C4min.deficitLE_lower`, `EFX.C4min.deficitLE_mono`, `EFX.C4min.omegaP_eq`, `EFX.C4min.omegaP_minFrozen_eq`, `EFX.C4min.omegaP_pos` | machine-checked in Lean (`formal/k4-dl2-descent`, PR #68). DL_R and DL₂ are hypotheses of these theorems, not axioms; DL₂ stays a conjecture (K4.STRAT.DL2), so K4.D and K4.T keep their status. Differences from the prose of `k4/strategy.md` §3, listed in the module doc: (1) the text takes a pre-allocation of least deficit in the finite min-frozen class; in Lean a pre-allocation is a base map G → Option A, so the proof descends on the deficit instead (after the first step, from a possible +∞, every deficit before the last is a positive integer and each step lowers it) and uses only that the class is nonempty (`EFX.C4min.exists_minFrozen`); (2) DL₂ is stated on connected cores, which gives `C4minROConn` (its 4-good-agent hypothesis is not used); the form `DefLocal2 → TheoremC4minRO` named in step 4 needs the statement on every core (`EFX.C4min.DefLocalAll`, `EFX.C4min.C4minRO_of_defLocalAll`); (3) "the bases differ" compares `baseOf goods` of the listed agents. The statement is parametrized by R so that a widened DL (DL_k with `BasesDiffer k`, or other moves) needs no new descent |

| K4.DL2.T13.LEAN | DL₁₃ (at f ≥ 1) ⟹ TARGET₄, with Theorem Z at f = 0, in Lean. The moves of `k4/dl2.md` §3 as relations on pre-allocations, each keeping the needed set NA: (T1) `EFX.C4min.MoveT1`, one listed agent y, free in P, takes another base B′_y ⊆ (B_y ∪ J) ∩ R_y and every other base is unchanged; (T3) `EFX.C4min.MoveT3`, a listed agent x frozen in P with base {g} and a listed agent z free in P with g ∈ N_z(B_z): z takes {g}, x takes a new base, and at most one further listed agent h ≠ x, z, free in P, takes a base missing at least one good of B_h, every other base unchanged. R₁₃ = (T1) ∪ (T3) (`EFX.C4min.R13`); R13Z = every pair of pre-allocations on a profile whose fewest frozen agents is 0 (some P ∈ 𝒫 has no frozen agent, `EFX.C4min.FewestFrozenZero`), R₁₃ otherwise (`EFX.C4min.R13Z`, the relation R* of `k4/dl2.md` §3); DL₁₃ (`EFX.C4min.DL13`) = DL for R₁₃ (`EFX.C4min.DefLocalAt`: if ω ≥ 1, every min-frozen P with def(P) > 0, +∞ included, has a min-frozen R₁₃-neighbour P′ with def(P′) < def(P)) on every strict profile of every connected k = 4 core whose every pre-allocation of 𝒫 has a frozen agent. At f = 0 Theorem Z gives DL for the full relation, so DL₁₃ ⟹ DL for R13Z (and conversely) ⟹ `C4minROConn` ⟹ TARGET₄. On the way: on 𝒫 the clause B′_y ⊆ (B_y ∪ J) ∩ R_y of (T1) is automatic; in (T3) x takes a new base; R₁₃ changes the bases of at most three agents | PROVED | `lean/EFX/DL13.lean` | `EFX.C4min.target4_of_DL13`, `EFX.C4min.C4minROConn_of_DL13`, `EFX.C4min.defLocal_R13Z_of_DL13`, `EFX.C4min.DL13_of_defLocal_R13Z`, `EFX.C4min.dl13_iff_defLocal_R13Z`, `EFX.C4min.defLocalAt_R13Z_of_f0`, `EFX.C4min.defLocalAt_top_of_f0`, `EFX.C4min.moveT1_of_inP`, `EFX.C4min.moveT3_new_base`, `EFX.C4min.moveT1_basesDiffer`, `EFX.C4min.moveT3_basesDiffer`, `EFX.C4min.r13_basesDiffer` | machine-checked in Lean (`formal/k4-dl13-frame`, PR #73). DL₁₃ is a hypothesis of these theorems, not an axiom; it stays a conjecture (K4.DL2.T13), so K4.D and K4.T keep their status. The relation follows the text of `k4/dl2.md` §3 ((T1) and (T3) with "the needed set NA staying the same"), equal to it, not widened; clauses the text derives (x takes a new base, x ≠ z, where the new bases lie) are left out of `MoveT3`, and the (T1) subset clause is kept and automatic on 𝒫. The code `R13` of `k4/dl2_relations.py` phrases the same moves by frozen-status changes instead of NA (no agent with an unchanged base changes its frozen status; in (T3) x unfreezes, z freezes with B′_z = B_x, the helper is free in P and P′); on pairs of min-frozen pre-allocations the two agree by Lemma 1(a), (b) of `k4/dl2.md` §4 and, for (T3), because NA is the set of the frozen agents' goods on 𝒫 and F(P′) = F(P) − x + z (the text's argument, not formalized). Theorem Z's Lean statement (`EFX.C4min.theoremZ_RO`) needs only a k = 4 core with f = 0, so it covers the f = 0 profiles of `EFX.C4min.DefLocal` (connected, strict) with no gap |
| K4.DL2.KEY.LEAN | The key frame of `k4/dl13.md` §2.3 (Remark "DL on the key graph") in Lean, for an arbitrary move relation M on pre-allocations (`EFX.C4min.Nbhd`). The key κ(P) (`EFX.C4min.key`): the needed set NA(P) and the frozen agents with their bases; def*(κ) (`EFX.C4min.KeyDeficitLE`, `EFX.C4min.KeyDeficitLT`): the least deficit of a min-frozen P of key κ, in ℤ ∪ {+∞}; N_M(κ) (`EFX.C4min.KeyNbr`): the keys of the min-frozen Q with M(P, Q) for some min-frozen P of key κ; R_key (`EFX.C4min.RKey` M): every pair on a profile whose fewest frozen agents is 0 (the clause of R13Z), otherwise κ(P′) ∈ N_M(κ(P)) or κ(P′) = κ(P); DL on the key graph (`EFX.C4min.DLKey` M): on every strict profile of every connected k = 4 core whose every pre-allocation of 𝒫 has a frozen agent, every key κ of a min-frozen P with ω ≥ 1 and def*(κ) > 0 (+∞ included) has a κ′ ∈ N_M(κ) with def*(κ′) < def*(κ). Then (i) DLKey M ⟹ DL for R_key (if def(P) > def*(κ(P)) a min-frozen state of the same key is better, otherwise one of a neighbouring key; at f = 0 Theorem Z), and conversely, so DLKey M ⟺ DL for R_key; (ii) DLKey M ⟹ `C4minROConn` ⟹ TARGET₄, for every M; (iii) M ⊆ M′ on pairs of min-frozen pre-allocations ⟹ (DLKey M ⟹ DLKey M′). On the way: def*(κ) is attained by a min-frozen P of key κ; DL for R ⟹ DL for R′ when R ⊆ R′ on min-frozen pairs; if a relation K keeps the key on min-frozen pairs, DL for K ∪ M at f ≥ 1 gives DLKey M at that profile. The Remark's point that the move may start from any state of the key, as the reduction repair lemmas plug into: if at every min-frozen P with ω ≥ 1 and def(P) = def*(κ(P)) > 0 some min-frozen state of the same key has an M-move to a min-frozen Q with def(Q) < def(P), or with def*(κ(Q)) < def*(κ(P)) (witnessed by any state of κ(Q)), then DLKey M holds at that profile | PROVED | `lean/EFX/KeyFrame.lean` | `EFX.C4min.target4_of_DLKey`, `EFX.C4min.C4minROConn_of_DLKey`, `EFX.C4min.defLocal_rKey_of_DLKey`, `EFX.C4min.defLocalAt_rKey_of_dlKeyAt`, `EFX.C4min.DLKey_of_defLocal_rKey`, `EFX.C4min.dlKeyAt_of_defLocalAt_rKey`, `EFX.C4min.dlKey_iff_defLocal_rKey`, `EFX.C4min.DLKey.mono`, `EFX.C4min.DLKeyAt.mono`, `EFX.C4min.KeyNbr.mono`, `EFX.C4min.dlKeyAt_of_defLocalAt_keep`, `EFX.C4min.dlKeyAt_of_defLocalAt`, `EFX.C4min.DefLocalAt.mono_minFrozen`, `EFX.C4min.rKey_of_move`, `EFX.C4min.rKey_of_key_eq`, `EFX.C4min.key_eq_iff`, `EFX.C4min.exists_least_keyDeficit`, `EFX.C4min.exists_keyMin`, `EFX.C4min.dlKeyAt_of_keyMin`, `EFX.C4min.dlKeyAt_of_keyMin_key` | machine-checked in Lean (`formal/k4-key-frame`, PR #79). DLKey M is a hypothesis of these theorems, not an axiom; no conjecture changes status, so K4.D and K4.T keep theirs. Readings where the Remark leaves room (module doc of the file): "every key κ" is every key of a min-frozen P (a key of no min-frozen P has def* = +∞ and no neighbour); the premise ω ≥ 1 of `EFX.C4min.DefLocalAt` is kept and is automatic (def*(κ) > 0 forces it); "f ≥ 1" is the negation of `EFX.C4min.FewestFrozenZero`, as in `EFX.C4min.DL13`; the key keeps the needed set although on 𝒫 it is the set of the frozen agents' goods; N_M(κ) asks the target of the move to be min-frozen too, which is harmless for R_C (every move of R_C keeps NA, and on 𝒫 the number of frozen agents is \|NA\|, `EFX.C4min.minFrozen_of_NA_eq`). With single (T3)/(T4) edges the key-graph form is refuted (K4.DL13.KEY, at the n = 5 failures of DL_RT4, K4.DL2.RT4, `results/k4_rt4/n5b_FAILURES.md`); with (T3⁺) ∪ (T4) edges it is the key-graph form of DL_RC (K4.DL2.RC; single-step DL_RC is refuted, the key-graph form has no known failure, K4.DL2.RCKEY), and the moves of R_C are plugged in by K4.DL2.RC.LEAN |
| K4.DL2.RC.LEAN | The moves of DL_RC (K4.DL2.RC, REFUTED by compute/k4-rc-refute), the successor of DL_RT4 (K4.DL2.RT4, REFUTED; data K4.DL2.RT4E), in Lean, and DL_RC ⟹ TARGET₄. (T2) rotation (`EFX.C4min.MoveT2`, `k4/dl2.md` §3): a set Y of at least two listed agents, free in P, takes new bases inside (J ∪ ⋃_{w ∈ Y} B_w) ∩ R_y, every other base and NA unchanged; (T4) (`EFX.C4min.MoveT4`, rows K4.DL2.T134, K4.DL2.RT4E): every changed agent is frozen in P and in P′, NA unchanged; (T3⁺) the frozen-chain role swap (`EFX.C4min.MoveT3plus`, as K4.DL2.RC defines it): NA unchanged; exactly one changed agent x goes from frozen to free; exactly one changed agent z goes from free to frozen and needs its new good in P; W (`EFX.C4min.ChangedFrozen`) = the changed agents frozen in both; the changed agents free in both are at most one, each giving up a good; the bases of W ∪ {z} in P′ are exactly the bases of W ∪ {x} in P. R_C = (T1) ∪ (T2) ∪ (T3⁺) ∪ (T4) (`EFX.C4min.RC`); R_T4 = (T1) ∪ (T2) ∪ (T3) ∪ (T4) (`EFX.C4min.RT4`) and DL_RT4 (`EFX.C4min.DLRT4`); DL_RC (`EFX.C4min.DLRC`) = DL for R_C on every strict profile of every connected k = 4 core whose every pre-allocation of 𝒫 has a frozen agent. | K4.DL2.RC | Conjecture DL_RC (the coordinator's successor of DL_RT4 after K4.DL2.RT4): for every strict profile of every connected k = 4 core whose fewest frozen agents is f ≥ 1, with ω ≥ 1, every min-frozen P ∈ 𝒫 with def(P) > 0 has a min-frozen P′ with def(P′) < def(P) reached by a move of R_C = (T1) ∪ (T2) ∪ (T3⁺) ∪ (T4), where (T3⁺), the frozen-chain role swap, is: NA(P′) = NA(P); exactly one changed agent x goes frozen → free; exactly one changed agent z goes free → frozen, and z needs its new good in P; W = the changed agents frozen in P and in P′; at most one helper, free in both, giving up a good of its base; and the bases of W ∪ {z} in P′ are exactly the bases of W ∪ {x} in P. (T3) is the case W = ∅, so R_T4 ⊆ R_C. **Fails at n = 5, f = 1 and f = 2** (`attempts/k4-rc-single-step.md`). (a) `rc-n5m13-f1`: pure core pos 4604 (m = 13, idx 58) of `results/k4_certs_5_pure.json.gz`, profile 44,118,8,8,158 (agents 0: 0:3, 2:5, 9:6, 11:7 / 1: 1:6, 6:5, 10:4, 12:8 / 2: 3:2, 7:3, 11:8, 12:4 / 3: 4:2, 8:3, 11:8, 12:4 / 4: 5:6, 9:4, 10:1, 12:8); P = ({11}, {12}, {3,7}, {4,8}, {5,9}), agent 0 frozen, NA = {11}, f = 1, ω = 4, def(P) = 1. At f = 1, R_C = R_T4. Lemma NP: every frozen agent is reached by a need path z, a_k, ..., a_1, x from a free z (each agent needing the next one's good), or the agents with a need walk to it are frozen and contain a cycle of the frozen need digraph; so at a T4-optimal P every frozen agent has such a path. Lemma 6⁺: the chain swap along a need path (x taking an admissible A, at most one helper re-basing) is min-frozen with the same needed set, and is a (T3⁺) move with W = {a_1, ..., a_k} when the helper (if any) gives up a good of its base. Lemma 8⁺: x's owner value after the swap, from P's data. Lemma 9⁺ and Corollary 9.1⁺ (the S1 repair with the owner at the free end of a need path, θ-ok along it); Lemma 11⁺ (contains `k4/dl13.md` Lemma 11 at k = 0) and Corollary 11.1⁺ (the blocker swap); Corollary 11.2⁺ (the κ swap; at k = 0 it is dl13 Lemma 11 with Y = X, κ = 1, a repair at part of the θ-b case); Corollary 8.2⁺ (the Lemma 7 swap; a (T3⁺) move only when the helper gives up a good of B_h, otherwise the bound holds for a role swap with a growing helper). Each bound is absolute, def(P′) ≤ ω + 2 − (a value from P), hence usable from any state of a key. Lemma CL: a (T4) move followed by a (T3⁺) move is a (T3⁺) move. Proposition N: where no (T4) move lowers def(P) > 0, frozen rotations reach a T4-optimal P* of equal deficit, and either (i) def*(κ(P*)) < def(P), a (T4) key edge to a smaller def* when P is at the T3 stage, or (ii) P* is at the T3 stage and its improving (T3⁺) moves lift to P; so the T3 stage may be assumed T4-optimal for DL on the key graph (not for single-step DL_RC). The same holds for the chain-end count δᵉ ≥ δ (Hall's deficiency against the unthreatened chain ends). | K4.SX.KEY | The key graph at one frozen agent (`k4/sx.md` §1–§2). Lemma 0: for every key κ (any f, ω ≥ 1), def*(κ) ≤ 0 iff some configuration at κ is completable (the per-key form of `k4/c4min.md` Lemma 1); a configuration with an owner valid with C = ∅ gives def(P_Q) ≤ 0. Remark 1.2: DL on the key graph at f = 1 implies, for every frozen good g of a key, that some key (g, ·) is completable, hence C₄ᵐⁱⁿ at f = 1 including the big-top case. Lemma F: at a configuration maximizing Theorem Z′'s (r′, Λ′) at a non-completable key (g, x), the configuration is pool-optimal, the threats among the free agents form a forest of out-trees with in-degree ≤ 1, its leaves are exactly the free-valid owners, every leaf threatens x, and every terminal has a threat path to a leaf. Lemma S: at a pool-optimal configuration no free agent that threatens no free agent threatens a terminal holding g | PROVED | `k4/sx.md`, `k4/sx_indep.py` | | written proof in `k4/sx.md` §1–§2, refereed in the PR #80 review. Lemma 0, Lemma F and Lemma S are asserted, with 0 violations, at every Z′-maximum counted in K4.SX.COV by `k4/sx_zprime.py` (model.py), and by the referee's `k4/sx_indep.py` (no repository code; Lemma 0 at every key, Lemma S at every pool-optimal configuration) on the samples listed in K4.SX.COV (`results/k4_sx/indep_*.log`) |
| K4.SX.REP | Repair lemmas at f = 1 (`k4/sx.md` §3). Each starts from a pool-optimal configuration Q at a key (g, x). A, B, B′ and C build a configuration at a key (g, z) with an owner valid with C = ∅, so def*(g, z) ≤ 0; C′ builds a state of deficit ≤ 0 directly (Lemma H1, unfreezing τ₁). **A**, owner swap, one (T3) move without helper: a terminal o threatens no free agent and threatens x, and o is not θ-b (big-top on g, its lower goods in its bundle, ω ≥ 2). **B**, generalized path move along a threat path τ = q₀ → … → q_k = o from a terminal to a leaf threatening x, with Lemma F's kinds and o not an (R) with its fourth good s_o in the pool: one (T3) move with helper o when k = 1; for k ≥ 2 it changes k + 2 agents, and its (T3)-adjacency to the start key is open. **B′**, the modified path move for an (R) leaf with s_o in the pool: o takes {a, s_o}; it assumes that X″ = (X_o ∖ {s_o}) ∪ {y} (y the other good of the threatener's pair) contains a pair for x, and (H_B′): X″ threatens no agent other than x and o (implied by (H_B′*): nobody but x values y); one (T3) move with helper o for k = 1. **C**: τ₁ a terminal with θ-b, o ≠ τ₁ a free agent threatening no free agent, P_x ⊆ X_{τ₁} a pair for x, robust for x and meeting U_{τ₁}, and (H): Q_o ∪ (X_{τ₁} ∖ P_x) threatens no free agent other than o and τ₁ (implied by (H*)); one (T3) move without helper. **C′**: exactly two terminals τ₁, τ₂, θ-b(τ₁), τ₂ threatening no free agent, P ⊆ X_{τ₁} a pair for x, robust, worth more than g, w ∈ U_{τ₁} ∖ P with U_{τ₂} ⊆ Y = (X_{τ₂} ∪ Q_{τ₁}) ∖ (P ∪ {w}), and (H′): Y threatens no free agent other than τ₁, τ₂ (implied by (H′*)); one (T3) move without helper. (T3)-adjacency for B and B′ with k ≥ 2 stays open (K4.SX.COVER). Each lemma is asserted against exact deficits wherever its hypotheses hold, with 0 violations: at every Z′-maximum of K4.SX.COV by `k4/sx_zprime.py`, and at every pool-optimal configuration of the samples of K4.SX.COV, for every pair of x, by the referee's `k4/sx_indep.py` (no repository code; `results/k4_sx/indep_*.log`). ## Lessons
- About 3,000 random cores "supported" conjecture A; exhaustive search refuted it in minutes (failures: 1 to 747 of 46,656 profiles per hypergraph, median 22; `results/fail_density_6_10.log`. An earlier sample of three hypergraphs showed 2 to 36).
- Every UNSAT claim that refutes a conjecture gets a second, independently written encoding before it enters this ledger.
- Delegating a range to an external theorem hid the smallest counterexamples. Bundles ≤ 2 already fail at n = 3, and conjecture A at n = 4, in the range m ≤ n + 3 that had been left to Mahara and never searched. Searching that range took 70 s.

5. ~~Conjecture D for disconnected cores in general~~ Done: Corollary D (`proofs/lb_last_step.md`) does not use connectivity; machine-checked in Lean (D).
6. ~~Read the sources in `proofs/citations.md`, including arXiv 2608.03171 and 2608.30203.~~ Done for seven papers (texts supplied by the repository owner): none implies TARGET or D; two give T3. Viswanathan–Mehta read too: ordinary EFX only (removed good must be worth > 0), so it does not imply TARGET. Still [unverified]: Christodoulou et al. (EC 2023), Chaudhury–Garg–Mehlhorn (JACM 2024), and the leads listed as not read.
7. ~~Conjecture D for β = 3 (m = 2n − 2, plan Step 3.2)~~ Done: D3 (certified; `proofs/beta3.md`). The collector theorem takes any number of collectors (D3.M), which settles every core without Q-agents at every β (D3.0); Q-agents are handled by Q-plans (D3.P), proved by hand for q ≤ 2 (D3.2) and checked on the 394 reduced β = 3 cores (D3.Q, D3.S). D for every β is now proved and machine-checked (D; `proofs/lb_last_step.md`, Corollary D).
8. S2.LB: D does not need it (S2.LB+, proved and machine-checked); what remains of S2.LB is that LB's lookahead never reaches the bad case of Theorem A (S2.R). ~~Lean formalization of Theorems 1′, A, B, C and Corollaries D, T~~ Done (`formal/lbplus`, PR #18).
9. (Moot for D and TARGET, which are proved; kept for the record.) A hand proof of D3.Q for q = 3, 4 (three or four agents without a private good), which would make D3 PROVED. These cases need dump targets (`proofs/beta3.md` §6).
10. (Moot for D and TARGET, which are proved; kept for the record.) Conjecture D for β = 4 (m = 2n − 3) by the same route: D3.P, D3.0 and the shortening lemma hold for every β, so it reduces to Q-plans for the reduced β = 4 cores, which have n ≤ 15 (D3.S). Enumerating all cores up to n = 15 with genbg is out of reach; generate the reduced cores from their kernels (≤ 6 branch vertices, ≤ 9 threads) instead. Whether Q-plans always exist for β ≥ 4 is open.
11. (Moot for D and TARGET, which are proved; kept for the record.) Minimal counterexample (plan Step 3.3, `proofs/min_counterexample.md`): TARGET was open only for β ≥ 4 before D, and a minimal counterexample satisfies β + 3 ≤ n ≤ 5β − 6 (MC4 with T3). Next: β = 4 leaves cores with 8 ≤ n ≤ 14 satisfying MC3 and MC6 (MC6 only where the Q-agent does not value the P-agent's other good); listing them needs a kernel-based generator (genbg lists all cores), and n = 8, m = 13 was not certified (now S2.N8). Reductions at Q-agents and at goods of degree ≥ 3 would be needed for unavoidability; the ones tried fail (`attempts/min-cex-pq-reductions.md`).
12. Multigraph extension (MX.*): prove MX.C (the dump in class T), handle U1 when a good with three or more valuers is the top of some valuers but not others (`attempts/multigraph_u1_mixed_top.md`), and find a replacement for the popular matching on profiles that have none (O1 in `proofs/multigraph_extension.md` §5).
13. Local search (`proofs/local_search.md`, rows LS1–LS6): a second, independent written proof of D and TARGET (Theorem C: two-phase local search with augmented envy cycles and a Hall-type junk placement). Needs an independent review and a re-implementation from the text; formalization candidates: Lemma 1 (partial EFX₀ in a core), Lemma 3 (threat bound), then Claims 1–5.
14. ~~(Optional) State `target` in Lean over any linearly ordered cancellative additive commutative monoid (covers ℝ≥0) by formalizing L12 (`proofs/real_values.md`).~~ Done (`formal/real-values`): `EFX.target_ordered` and `EFX.corollaryD_ordered` over any `EFX.OrderedValue` (`lean/EFX/RealValues.lean`), via `EFX.l12`.
15. k = 4 (rows K4.*, `k4/SCOUT.md`): route LB₄ (`k4/SCOUT.md` §5): implement LB with the k = 4 upgrade and owner rules and run it on every strict profile of every core with n ≤ 3 (needs C: 288³ profiles per core), record its smallest failing configurations, prove its soundness (Theorem 1 analogue), state and test the analogue of S2.K (the large bundle holds private pairs), then redo Theorem A and the rotation. Computation: every core with n ≤ 5 that has a 4-good agent (all-3-good cores: row D), and with n = 6 and exactly one, is certified (K4.R5c, K4.R5d, K4.R5p, K4.R6a; compute/k4-frontier, `k4/frontier/README.md`); next: n = 6 with two 4-good agents (119,283 cores, `results/k4_frontier_counts_6.log`; ≈ 24 CPU-hours with `k4/frontier/search.py --tries=2`, plus checking; not planned: effort goes to LB₄ʳ), then three or more. Constructions can be tested against every certified core and profile with `k4/frontier/tester.py` (C plugin, pipe or oracle) (LB₄: see item 18.)
16. k = 4 minimal counterexample (rows K4.MC*, `k4/MINCEX.md`): TARGET₄ for β ≤ 3 (K4.MC6, certified) and β ≤ 4 (K4.MC7, certified; uses the multigraph theorem for 6 graphical all-P4 cores with n = 6, m = 15). Next: certify those 6 directly (288⁶ profiles each; one ran an hour without finishing; symmetry or a k = 4 proof of the multigraph theorem for cores, as T5 did for k = 3, would do); β = 5 (n ≤ 12 by K4.MC4); two-agent gadgets (a C reducer) for the configurations that persist: excess agents adjacent to one another, and Q4 agents. Certifying the closed-xy reduction (two P4 agents sharing two goods of degree 2: one-agent gadgets reduce 73,548 of 82,944 pair profiles, `attempts/k4-mincex-xy-pairs.md`) would cut the domains of 4 of the 6 uncertified cores
17. k = 4 local search (rows K4.LS.*, `k4/local_search4.md`): the k = 3 scheme with arbitrary choices fails at n = 4, m = 7 (dead end, K4.LS.DEAD). A local-search proof of TARGET₄ needs a choice rule that provably avoids dead ends, or moves that can lower some agent's value (as LB₄'s rotation can). Neither simple rule tried does it (default and `-DALT` both fail on about 1 in 10⁶ pure n = 4 profiles). Also open: review Theorem 1, Lemma 5 and Propositions 3, 4, 7; polynomial-time search for exchange cycles and splits; whether dead ends exist at n = 3 (none in 1,020,000 samples; exhaustive needs about 13 CPU-hours with `k4/ls4_deadend.c`). Positive direction (`k4/ls4plus.md`, rows K4.LSP.*): LS4⁺ adds level-sum-raising coalition re-divisions; it had no failure in any run (K4.LSP.RUN, evidence), and its correctness would follow from conjecture GM₄ (maxima of the level sum admit a placement; equivalently, no dead end maximizes the level sum). GM₄ is refuted (K4.GM.CEX, `k4/gm4.md`), and so is its existence form (K4.GM.E): no argument that takes a maximum of the level sum (with any tie-break) and places its pool proves TARGET₄. LS4⁺_n can fail under some choices; its default rule has no known failure, but its correctness cannot be proved through GM₄ or GM₄∃. Next: a proof that the default rule stops at a placeable state; or the existence form for the convex potentials Σ 2^ℓ or leximax (K4.GM.POT, `k4/gm4.md` §6); or a bounded move set with another potential.
18. LB₄ (`k4/lb4.md`, rows K4.LB4*; follows item 15's LB₄ route): LB₄ never fails on the certified cores, but K4.LB4 is open. Needed for a proof: a Theorem A₄ (count the goods that frozen agents and agents with two-good bases need kept out of the large bundle against the free slots; Lemma 2₄ protects the agents with a pick base only when their own slots take their best junk goods, which competes with those counted goods), a Theorem B₄ for LB₄'s rotation, and a rule or exchange argument for the insertion sequence (with one rotation a fixed order fails, and so does searching only the last block's leader, even from the index order: `attempts/lb4-fixed-insertion.md`, `attempts/lb4-last-block-leader.md`). Index insertion with up to three nested rotations and every upgrade policy (`-i0 -u3 -r3 -w1 -c1`) fails nowhere on any certified core, n ≤ 4 and n = 5 with at most two 4-good agents (`results/k4_lb4_nested_n4.log`, `results/k4_lb4_nested_pure4.log`, `results/k4_lb4_nested_n5.log`), and for every insertion order on n ≤ 3 and on n = 4 with at most three 4-good agents (`results/k4_lb4_nested_every.log`): a candidate Theorem C₄ (every run of Phase 1 works after upgrades and at most three rotations; false on H_5). Row K4.LB4R (`k4/lb4.md` §5, "LB₄ʳ under stress"): no failure for every order on pure n = 4 (exhaustive), on n = 5 to 7 (sampled) and under hill-climbing toward hard profiles; no run with a rotation histogram (`-d2`, n ≤ 7) needs a third rotation once every policy is allowed (but H_4, n = 17, needs three under every policy at index order), and envy-free upgrades alone with at most two rotations fail nowhere tested (need-shrinking alone fails: `attempts/lb4r-need-shrinking-only.md`); but the cores H_t of `k4/c4.md` §7 (PR #33, Proposition H, confirmed by an independent second encoding; n = 4t + 1) need ⌈2t/3⌉ rotations at index order, so no bounded "every run" form of C₄ holds, and the open question is whether some insertion order keeps the rotations bounded (evidence in `k4/lb4.md` §5, "Some insertion order": on H_1–H_3 most random orders need no rotation with `-w0`, an upper bound for LB₄ʳ's `-w1`; up to n = 7 no profile found on which every order needs two); adaptive insertion (`k4/adaptive.md`, rows K4.AD.*): choosing only the first agent by lookahead (rule F) keeps LB₄ʳ at one rotation on every profile tested (n ≤ 4 exhaustive except pure n = 4, sampled there and on n = 5, hill-climbing to n = 8), which is optimal on n ≤ 4 with at most three 4-good agents; on H_t it needs none on every relabeling (Proposition H′, proved, refereed in the #44 review); but rule F is false in general: Lemma M is false (HH_3, K4.LMBT.M, PR #83), and on HH_t a single first agent needs a number of rotations growing with t (Corollary HH; K4.LMBT.COR, not refereed); the adaptive repair M_ad (the agent chosen at every insertion step) is implied by K4.LB4 (see PR #77); still to run: index order on n = 5 with three or more 4-good agents exhaustively (three: done, all 9,861 cores, no failure), every order on the certified n = 5 cores. Also: review K4.LB4.SH (K4.LB4.S is machine-checked, `lean/EFX/PreAllocK.lean`); test LB₄ on n = 5 with three or more 4-good agents; the analogue of S2.K (what the large bundle contains). Theorem C₄ (`k4/c4.md`, rows K4.C4.*): the candidate is false as stated: on the gadget chain H_t, LB₄ʳ with index insertion needs ⌈2t/3⌉ nested rotations, so it fails on H_5 with three, and for every fixed insertion order τ and every bound R, LB₄ʳ(τ) with R rotations fails on a relabeled copy of H_t (order-keeping relabeling; H_5 for R = 3) (K4.C4.R, PROVED; the candidate is row K4.C4.C, REFUTED). LB⁺'s Theorems A and B carry over except at exposed 4-good agents, and one exposed 4-good agent or few kept-out goods are partly handled (K4.C4.AB, awaiting review): they prove LB₄ʳ's success on 78–95% of the runs that need an owner (n ≤ 4); the rest is K4.C4.GAP. Next: prove C₄¹∃ (K4.C4.1: at most one 4-good agent; one rotation is not enough at n = 5, two suffice on the data; on the data C₄¹∃ reduces to the Exchange Lemma X on insertion sequences, K4.C4.1X, for which Lemmas Ω and Ψ, K4.C4.OM and K4.C4.PSI (PROVED), give a key-decreasing move, a step of the weaker Lemma X* over states, on 62–90% of the open runs at n ≤ 5; #33's one-rotation form C₄¹ is refuted, K4.C4.1R), and for K4.LB4 either a rule for the insertion sequence or a potential that bounds unboundedly many rotations Rule F made explicit (`k4/rulef.md`, rows K4.RF.*): rule RK takes the first agent whose run Lemma K (an owner count with kept goods and served agents, written proof) certifies, before or after one rotation, or which satisfies C₄⁰; it leaves no profile open on n ≤ 4 with at most three 4-good agents (exhaustive), on samples of pure n = 4 and n = 5, on H_t and on the suite, so rule F with at most one rotation reduces to Lemma M (some first agent in K0 or K1), which is false (HH_3); Lemma K′, its exact form, puts K4.AD.F itself in counting form; `lean/EFX/RuleF.lean` states rule F's target (`TheoremRuleF`, `RuleFConn`, `RuleFOne`) and proves it gives TARGET₄.
19. k4 Hall route (`k4/hall.md`, rows K4.HALL.*; second attempt at C₄ᵐⁱⁿ of `k4/c4x.md`): proved in writing and refereed twice (PR #46; rows PROVED, not in Lean): the covering form, the exposure lemmas H3, H6, H7, Theorem H0 (C₄ᵐⁱⁿ when some Pareto-maximal pre-allocation without frozen agents has two one-good holders, or some π-cycle without a label collision) and the H_t family. Open: the label collision (conjecture K4.HALL.F0S, level-sum maxima). K4.HALL.BT (the only other obstruction is a frozen big-top agent) is false at n = 4 (bt4, `attempts/k4-hall-bt-n4.md`, repaired by a downgrade swap). When an exposed frozen big-top agent exists, some exchange cycle through it completes on every such maximum with n ≤ 3, but conjecture K4.HALL.BTCYC (`k4/hall_bt.md`) is false at n = 4 (PR #53, `attempts/k4-gap-btcyc.md`, pure core 122; repaired by a downgrade swap). Lemma BT1 is proved. Next: find a move catalogue (exchange cycles plus downgrade swaps) that covers every exposed frozen agent.
20. K4.D stress test (rows K4.DS.*, `k4/d_stress.md` §4): D2 held on 5,400 random profiles of 21 structured cores (chains H_t, t ≤ 7; multi-head chains; cycles; trees; pure cores) and no failure is known. Not done: gadgets of other sizes and shapes, and gadgets sharing goods; exhaustive certification beyond n = 5 (H_2 alone has ≈ 1.06·10²⁰ profiles); an owner metric that counts an owner only when its bundle can have more than 2 goods, climbed on H_3+, the trees and the multi-head chains; an adversarial objective with a real gradient (e.g. the least size of the large bundle versus m − 2n + 2); pure cores with m > 2n.
21. C₄ᵐⁱⁿ (`k4/c4min.md`, rows K4.C4MIN.*). Theorem Z (fewest frozen agents 0; covers every H_t) is PROVED: written proof refereed on PR #41, main claim in Lean (K4.C4MIN.Z.LEAN). Lemma 1 (configurations, both directions), Theorem F (some min-frozen configuration has only robust frozen agents) and the k = 3 reduction to K3 have written proofs, refereed on PR #41 with fixes; Theorem F and Lemma 1(a) are being formalized in PR #55. Z and F together cover 94% of the n = 3 profiles with ω ≥ 1 and 56–89% at n = 4. Conjecture Φ′ is refuted at n = 4, f = 2 (PR #53), so the rest (every configuration has an exposed frozen agent, all of f = 1 among them) needs another potential or an existence form; at f = 1, Φ′ is not refuted (roadmap in §4). Next: verify the review fixes (K4.C4MIN.CFG, F, K3); a potential or move catalogue for exposed frozen agents, starting with f = 1.
22. Induction on the number of 4-good agents (rows K4.IND.*, `k4/induct.md`, proof/k4-induct). The insertion lemma as posed fails (K4.IND.X). For a private good, the step is exactly "X′ with w unenvied" (K4.IND.INS, Lemma 2). So the P4 and PP4 agents of a minimal counterexample in 𝒟_{j+1} reduce to PS(I − p, w) (K4.IND.STEP, Theorem 4). Next: prove PS for the k ≤ 3 instances I − p, e.g. by LBO (K4.IND.LBO: LB⁺ with the target last). Its open part is choosing the run when the target ends Phase 1 without its top, cf. PR #37's Lemma X (open, not merged). Also: find an insertion rule for Q4 agents (K4.IND.Q4; the every-minimizer rules fail, their existence forms are open); test LBO on the PP4 cores.
23. C₄ᵐⁱⁿ at f = 1 by reduction to Theorem Z (`k4/c4min_reduce.md`, rows K4.C4MIN.RED.*). Theorem Z holds on I′ = I − x − g at every key, and reinsertion reduces to a counting condition (Lemma C); both refereed. But every black-box or single-key version of the reduction fails (K4.C4MIN.RED.X), so the key must be optimized together with the configuration. Next: prove K4.C4MIN.RED.LIL (broad catalogue; the narrow one of Lemma R(iii) and #50 fails at n = 3 and, with recycling, at n = 4) or K4.C4MIN.RED.GLOB. Needed at a non-completable configuration or maximum (`k4/c4min_reduce.md` §5, §5.2–§5.3): t = 0 (for t = 1 with a big-top x the core's private-goods rule is necessary, instance NC); threat-injectivity despite pool improvements blocked by t (instance B1); the case r′ ≤ |D_x|, i.e. r′ = 0 with every vertex threatened, r′ = 1 through the need edge x → z (a path w → … → x, w unthreatened but not necessarily robust, and the only terminal being w), and |D_x| = 2 for a flat x or c + d < a < b + d.
24. K3 algorithm (rows K3.ALG, K3.ALG.TIME, K3.OWNER, K3.ALG.RUN; `proofs/k3_algorithm.md`, workstream `formal/k3-algo`). Done: algorithm K3ALG (peel by R1, then LB⁺) with correctness (`EFX.K3.algo_efx0`) and a running-time bound of 400·(n + m + 1)⁴ counted operations (`EFX.K3.algoC_cost`), both machine-checked; LB⁺'s owner test needs no minimum vertex cover (Proposition O, `EFX.LB.validOwner_iff`). Done (`formal/k3-extras`, rows K3.LASTBLOCK, K3.SIZE, K3.SD2, K3.RAT): r lies in the last block, the size of the large bundle, serial dictatorship for k ≤ 2 and the rational-value corollary, machine-checked (`lean/EFX/K3Extras.lean`). Done (`formal/k3-real-cost`): the finer bound O(n⁴ + n²m) (row K3.ALG.FINE, `EFX.K3.algoC_cost_fine`) and K3ALG on real values in the comparison model via L12's computed surrogate (row K3.ALG.REAL, `EFX.K3.algoOrd_efx0`, `EFX.K3.algoOrdC_cost`). Open: machine-check the O((n + m) log n) implementation `fast` (`k3/k3algo.py`, §5); the §3 remark that K3ALG run directly on v makes the same decisions as on w (not needed for the comparison-model corollary); whether the owner test is hard for an arbitrary owner (not needed by LB⁺). Paper follow-up done (`paper/k3-lean-update`): `paper/k3` (both versions) now labels these results Lean-checked (status tables, the last-block remark, the bundle-size proposition, k ≤ 2 for ℕ, "nonnegative" rationals, Cor. real in the comparison model as K3.ALG.REAL states it, the finer bound as layer (ii)), with the new names in the Lean index and the shorter "not formalized" lists; the cited repository commit is c6d1871.
25. C₄ᵐⁱⁿ with one frozen agent (`k4/c4min_f1.md`, rows K4.C4MIN.F1*; builds on item 21). Theorem F1 (written proof, refereed in the PR #50 review; PROVED, not in Lean) covers every f = 1 profile with a Ψ-maximum whose frozen agent is not big-top, and big-top frozen agents when ω = 1. For 4-good frozen agents it needs K4.C4MIN.F1E (the double case (E′), n ≥ 7; no computational evidence, since no run has n ≥ 7). Open: (a) the double case (E′); (b) the big-top case with ω ≥ 2, where no potential starting with (r, Λ) works and Φ_BT fails (K4.C4MIN.F1BT). The strategy session (proof/k4-strategy) now decides how C₄ᵐⁱⁿ is pursued, so no further potentials are proposed here.
26. k = 4 exposed-frozen gap (rows K4.GAP.*, `k4/gap.md`, suite `results/k4_gap/instances_v1.json`): every potential-maximum form (Φ′, K4.GAP.PHI) and every local-improvement catalogue of #41 §4 and #52 fails at n = 4, the latter where only a change of the needed set helps. BTCYC and BT are refuted. What survives on all data: C₄ᵐⁱⁿ in configuration form, and reachability by exchange cycles and downgrade swaps from each Pareto-maximum (REACH_EACH). A proof needs a statement of that reachability kind, or moves that change the needed set. The strategy session (proof/k4-strategy) reuses the suite via `gb.check_instances`.
27. k = 4 strategy (`k4/strategy.md`, rows K4.STRAT.*; the suite `k4/suite/`). No candidate of the forms "every maximum of a potential is completable", "a fixed move catalogue raises a proxy potential" or "a counting certificate" survives the suite. The named target was Conjecture DL₂ (the deficit has no local minimum above 0 for two-agent base changes at the fewest frozen agents), now refuted (K4.DL2.X3; its successors DL_T and DL₁₃ and the next steps on that line are item 28), and rank 2 LB₄ʳ with rule F (#44). Next: (1) kill-or-confirm DL₂ exhaustively on n ≤ 3 and n = 4 with one or two 4-good agents (a C version of `k4/suite/deficit_local.py`; #53's `gap.c -D` already has the deficit), and on H₂, H₃ and random n = 5, 6 profiles; (2) classify the two-agent repairs by the obstruction type (Lemma H3's e1–e3, Lemma H7's G/G1/L) to get the proof's case list; (3) prove the one-agent repairs; (4) Lean: done (K4.STRAT.DL2.LEAN, PR #68): DL_R ⟹ `C4minROConn` ⟹ TARGET₄ for every neighbourhood relation R, in particular DL₂ ⟹ TARGET₄, by finite descent with `EFX.C4min.DeficitLE`; on every core, DL_R ⟹ `TheoremC4minRO`. Every new candidate statement should first pass `python3 k4/suite/run.py --expected` and be run on the suite. Rank 2 (rule F, `k4/rulef.md`): reduced to Lemma M (K4.RF.M), and Lemma M is false (HH_3, K4.LMBT.M, PR #83): no single first agent works with one rotation, and with R rotations rule F fails on HH_t for t > 2R + 1 (Corollary HH; K4.LMBT.COR, not refereed); its big-top step fails on H_3 + q (K4.LMBT.A). The adaptive repair M_ad (the agent chosen at every insertion step) is implied by K4.LB4 (see PR #77).
28. DL₂ and its successors DL_T and DL₁₃ (`k4/dl2.md`, rows K4.DL2.*, proof/k4-dl2-k1). DL₂ is refuted at n = 3, m = 7 (K4.DL2.X3): a deficit trap whose nearest repair is a role swap of a big-top frozen agent and its needer plus a helper giving up one of the frozen agent's lower goods. Since PR #68 any DL_R implies TARGET₄. The relation R_T (re-base keeping the needed set; rotation among free agents; role swap with a needer and at most one helper) survived every state tested before #74 (K4.DL2.TE); it fails at f = 3 (K4.DL2.T); it is local only in the frozen agents (re-bases and rotations are the moves within a key), so DL_T holds at f = 0 by Theorem Z and its open content is f ≥ 1. There the sharper DL₁₃ (re-bases and role swaps only, no rotations; K4.DL2.T13) is refuted at n = 4 (compute/k4-dl13, #74), though it holds at every f ≥ 1 state with n ≤ 3 (K4.DL2.T13N); its smallest counterexample (n = 4, m = 6, f = 3: the only improvements exchange frozen goods between frozen agents) contradicts DL_T as well (`attempts/k4-dl13-frozen-swap.md`, issue #76), so DL_T is refuted at f ≥ 1 too (K4.DL2.T; it holds at f = 0 by Theorem Z); other relations fail (K4.DL2.RX). Proved in writing and refereed (K4.DL2.MOVES, K4.DL2.DEF): every R_T move stays in the min-frozen class; the exact one-agent deficit criteria (Lemmas 2, 3; on the data they certify all but one of the 39,466 one-agent repairs); the extension through any move and the unfreezing mechanism of the traps (Lemmas 2*, 7). These lemmas and Lemma H1 are machine-checked (K4.DL2.MOVES.LEAN, K4.DL2.DEF.LEAN, K4.HALL.H1.LEAN, `lean/EFX/DL2Moves.lean`), with the agreement of the text's (T1), (T3) and the code's. Next: (1) a deficit lemma for role swaps at f ≥ 1 (when does the swap, with its helper, make the unfrozen agent a better owner? Lemma 7 gives the unfreezing, not the safety); (2) an existence argument for DL₁₃: at a def > 0 state with f ≥ 1 and no (T1) repair, a (T3) repair (no obstruction class forces a one-agent repair, `k4/dl2.md` §2.1); (3) ~~Lean: the relation "(T1) or (T3) when f ≥ 1, everything when f = 0" with Theorem Z, so that DL₁₃ ⟹ TARGET₄ is machine-checked~~ Done (K4.DL2.T13.LEAN, `EFX.C4min.target4_of_DL13`); (4) done (K4.DL2.T13N): DL₁₃ at every n ≤ 3 state, on n = 4, 5, 6 data, refuted at n = 4. Steps (1)–(3) assumed DL₁₃; a successor relation must contain exchanges of frozen goods among frozen agents (or need chains of length two) and rotations of three free agents (or role swaps with two helpers), `attempts/k4-dl13-refuted.md` §3. R_13 plus frozen-good permutations alone fails (K4.DL2.T134); R_T plus them (DL_RT4) survived every state tested at n ≤ 4 (K4.DL2.RT4E); refuted at n = 5 (K4.DL2.RT4); the target was DL_RT4, with T4 needed only at f = 3 and T2 only with several free agents on the n ≤ 4 data. proof/k4-dl13 (PR #75, `k4/dl13.md`, rows K4.DL13.*): (1) done (written proofs, refereed in the PR #75 review): Lemma 8 gives the unfrozen agent's owner value after a role swap exactly from P's data, Lemma 11 bounds an unmoved owner's (K4.DL13.SWAP); Lemma 12: Pareto rotations of the frozen goods never raise the deficit (K4.DL13.ROT). Lemmas 8–12 with their corollaries are machine-checked (K4.DL13.SWAP.LEAN, K4.DL13.ROT.LEAN, `lean/EFX/DL13Moves.lean`). (2) partial: three repairs with structural hypotheses (the S1 repair, the blocker swap, the Lemma 7 swap) certify 89% of 13,971 T1-stuck state records, and Lemmas 8 and 11 account for some repair at every T1-stuck state (K4.DL13.E); T1-stuckness is restated and its consequences derived (written proof, refereed in the PR #75 review; K4.DL13.STUCK); the natural structure lemmas fail (K4.DL13.X), and so does the repaired target DL₁₃^opt (K4.DL13.OPT, at f = 1). DL₁₃ itself is refuted by compute/k4-dl13 (n = 4); the target was DL for R_T4 = (T1) ∪ (T2) ∪ (T3) ∪ (T4), equivalent to its T3 stage, where Conjecture SX (a frozen agent is exposed) has no failure (K4.DL13.SX). Open: SX (via Theorem Z′ on the free part of a key), the θ-b case, and the one-needer regime's helper (`k4/dl13.md` §6). compute/k4-rt4-n5: DL_RT4 is refuted at n = 5, f = 3 (K4.DL2.RT4: 67 states in 10 sampled profiles of 5 cores, `attempts/k4-rt4-n5-chain.md`), and so is DL on the key graph with single (T3)/(T4) edges (K4.DL13.KEY): at all 67 failures found, every nearest repair is a frozen chain, a role swap whose good passes through a frozen agent. The next target is DL_RC, R_C = (T1) ∪ (T2) ∪ (T3⁺) ∪ (T4) with the frozen-chain role swap (T3⁺) in place of (T3), or its key-graph form with (T3⁺) ∪ (T4) edges (K4.DL2.RC, then a CONJECTURE with no failure on these profiles or on the suite; refuted since, see below); compute/k4-rc builds its checkers at scale and formal/k4-key-frame the Lean frame. Lean: the frame `EFX.C4min.target4_of_defLocal` takes any relation, so nothing in Lean changes. Single-step DL is refuted for R_C too (K4.DL2.RC: cores 4604 and 4515 of `k4_certs_5_pure`, n = 5, f = 1 and 2; also with an unrestricted helper, K4.DL2.RCY), while its key-graph form held at every failing state (K4.DL2.RCKEY; compute/k4-rc-refute). The live conjectures are the key-graph covering statements K4.SX.COVER (f = 1) and K4.F2.COVER (f ≥ 2, PR #82) and the uniform Theorem ZMOVE, being attacked on proof/k4-zmove-hall and proof/k4-zmove-pot.
